Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_medium': channel width 300, device koios_medium, seed 1) has a critical path of 12.4624 ns and uses 219 DSP tiles of the device's 770, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.53973e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.18888e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in a different my_fir instance (endpoint group $dffe~788, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 12.297 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Observed across 12 timing reports through 2026-09-20; block, clock-to-Q and packing rows are from the architecture file and exact; wire and adder rows are sampled ranges with the median in parentheses.

element on a pathns
block, any mode2.14
wire, block to block0.96 to 2.22 (median 1.17)
wire, register to first block1.0 to 3.82 (median 2.25)
wire, last block to register0.83 to 2.67 (median 2.25)
wire, block into soft logic0.75 to 3.67 (median 1.48)
soft adder level, cell plus wire to the next0.65 to 2.57 (median 1.08)
clock-to-Q0.06

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

TIER: T1 | T2 | NONE
REGIONS: <comma-separated region id(s)> # one id for a direct swap; N ids for a fusion
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal/module the terms feed> # or NONE for a single region
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, a single integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, a single integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage your rewrite leaves, in ns, a single number>
MAPPING: <which operands map to which of the mode's ports>
PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum in each my_fir_* module (stereovision2.v:1486, 1542, 1602, 1661, 1716, 1775, 1833) — the single 28-bit tap-sum registered into dout and driven out as h_fltr's vidin_out_temp_*
BLOCKS: 144
TILES: 226
STAGE_NS: 8.09
MAPPING: per block, ax = the 16-bit unsigned tap sample (din or n_delay_reg1..6) zero-extended to 18 bits; ay = that tap's constant coefficient zero-extended to 19 bits (largest is 255, well inside the 18/19-bit signed ranges); bx/by = the second tap/coefficient of the pair, same extension; result[27:0] is the pair's exact partial sum and feeds the soft adder tree into the existing dout flip-flop. Concretely, for the 7-term FIRs (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2 and h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg3, by=C3
U2: ax=n_delay_reg4, ay=C4, bx=n_delay_reg5, by=C5
U3: ax=n_delay_reg6, ay=C6, bx=19'd0 wired as bx=18'd0, by=19'd0 (unused term tied to 0; no port left unconnected)
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + (U2[27:0] + U3[27:0]);
For the 6-term FIRs (f3: 12,77,148,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,193,25,15; h3 and h4: 9,56,109,109,56,9 — the C3 tap is a literal 0 coefficient and has no term in the source):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=C4
U2: ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=C5, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=C6
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + U2[27:0];
PLAN:

What is converted. All 7 my_fir_* modules, each instantiated once inside h_fltr, which is instantiated 6 times (stereovision2.v:188-190, 195-197) — hence the 6 instances per region id. Per h_fltr: 4+4+4 blocks for the three 7-term FIRs (f1, f2, h2) and 3+3+3+3 for the four 6-term FIRs (f3, h1, h3, h4) = 24 blocks; ×6 instances = 144 blocks.

In f2 and h2 the two ×4 taps (lines 1542/1548 and 1716/1722) are not in the candidate list because synthesis currently implements them as shifts. They are terms of the same dout reduction, so they go onto block ports as coefficient 19'd4. That is exactly what the source writes; nothing is fabricated.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned; zero-extended to the 18-bit signed ax/bx they span 0..65535, inside the port's +131071 maximum (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). Coefficients are positive literals ≤ 255 on the 19-bit signed ay/by. Every product ≤ 65535·255 = 16 711 425; a block's two-product sum ≤ 33 422 850, exact in result[64]; the full 7-term sum ≤ 116 979 975 < 2^27, so the 28-bit dout and the 28-bit soft tree hold it without truncation, and the original 32-bit Verilog expression truncated to dout[27:0] loses nothing either. Integer addition is associative, so re-associating the 6 adds into a 2-level tree is bit-identical. No product is truncated or registered before the sum in these modules, so the "fused sum truncated after" hazard does not apply.

Live values and control preserved. dout keeps its own flip-flop and its if (new_data_rdy) enable — the blocks only replace the combinational expression driving it, so no register behaviour is deleted and module latency is unchanged (dout is still valid one cycle after the inputs, in step with output_data_ready). The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its enable, and output_data_ready are untouched. din and the delay registers stay real signals read by the shift chain; a block only takes a copy of an operand, so observability is preserved. Module ports and h_fltr's vidin_out_reg_* capture logic are unchanged; steer_fltr sees the same values.

Numbers required before committing.

  • Blocks: 144. Tiles: a comb_sop_2_18 takes its tile alone, so 144 tiles for the blocks. The 246 my_fir multiplies that currently sit two-to-a-tile disappear; the 252 fltr_compute 9×9 multiplies stay. Conservatively assuming the freed tiles are only those holding my_fir multiplies exclusively and no repacking of mixed tiles, the design lands at ≈226 tiles (+7 over the baseline 219); if the 9×9 multiplies repack four to a tile it is ≈207. Either way it is far inside the device's 770.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. The mode has no addend or chain port and a partial sum already needs 25 bits, so it cannot re-enter an 18-bit ax; partial sums are combined in soft logic. There is no block-to-block hop to charge.
  • Stage delay, median table values: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block-into-soft-logic wire 1.48 + two soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns, against the 12.4624 ns baseline critical path. Even taking the worst sampled wires on both hops (3.82 and 3.67) with median adders the stage is 11.85 ns, still below the 12.297 ns second path.
  • Soft logic leaving the touched stage: 4 of the 6 28-bit adder levels plus the final LUT in each 7-term FIR (3 of 5 levels in each 6-term FIR), i.e. roughly 110 carry-chain cells per FIR instance across 42 FIR instances. This is the slowest stage, and the second-slowest stage is the same shape in another my_fir instance — both are covered because every my_fir_* module and therefore every instance of that shape is converted. That is the reason the conversion is applied to all 7 modules rather than to the single reported critical path.
  • Expected ADP: ≈9.72e7 area (conservative 226 tiles, counting no cluster savings) × 8.09 ns ≈ 7.9e8, against the 1.18888e9 baseline — about 34% lower. Even if some untouched stage turns out to sit near 9.5 ns and caps the clock there, ADP is ≈9.2e8, still below baseline.

What is left, and why.

  • fltr_compute_f1 (ids 44-50), fltr_compute_f3 (72,73,74,76,77,78), fltr_compute_h4 (128-134) — 20 regions: the design registers each product in a 17-bit q and then sign-extends q[16] into the 20-bit sum (e.g. stereovision2.v:867). Their largest coefficients overflow that: 277·255=70635 (f1), 364·255=92820 (f3), 324·255=82620 (h4), all ≥ 2^16, so those products wrap negative before the sum. A fused block sums the untruncated products and produces a different value — "original truncates each product before the sum → no". Leave.
  • fltr_compute_f2 (59-63), fltr_compute_h1 (86-92), fltr_compute_h2 (101-105), fltr_compute_h3 (114-120) — 22 regions: these never exceed 65025, so fusion would be exact, but converting them cannot lower the clock. Their products are separated from the reduction by the q1..q7 pipeline registers (din→q→d_out_tmp→dout), so a combinational fusion would have to either delete a pipeline stage or re-introduce partial-sum registers, and in either case the three modules above stay unconverted and keep the identical 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage. A rewrite that leaves the governing stage of that class in place buys no period and is paid for in tiles alone (rule 1). Leave.

Risks. (1) The 2-level adder tree depends on the explicit parenthesisation ((U0+U1)+(U2+U3)) surviving synthesis; if the tool linearises it the stage grows by one adder level to ≈9.2 ns, still well under baseline. (2) Tile count is an estimate: the baseline packing of the 9×9 fltr_compute multiplies is not derivable from the source, so post-rewrite tiles could be anywhere in ≈207-226; the ADP win holds across that range. (3) If a stage outside my_fir — most plausibly the 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage in fltr_compute — is slower than 8.09 ns it becomes the new critical path and caps the gain; it cannot make the design slower than baseline, since no stage is lengthened. (4) No new operation or datatype family is introduced: the same integer products and the same integer sum, re-associated.

I'll read the source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1486$154, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1487$155, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1488$157, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1489$159, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1490$161, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1491$163, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1492$165, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1543$170, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1544$172, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1545$174, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1546$176, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1547$178, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1602$184, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1603$185, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1604$187, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1605$189, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1606$191, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1607$193, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1661$197, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1662$198, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1663$200, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1664$202, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1665$204, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1666$206, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1717$211, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1718$213, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1719$215, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1720$217, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1721$219, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1775$225, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1776$226, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1777$228, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1778$230, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1779$232, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1780$234, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1833$238, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1834$239, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1835$241, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1836$243, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1837$245, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1838$247
SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum in each my_fir_* module (stereovision2.v:1486, 1542, 1602, 1661, 1716, 1775, 1833) — the single 28-bit tap-sum registered into dout and driven out as h_fltr's vidin_out_temp_*
BLOCKS: 144
TILES: 226
STAGE_NS: 8.09
MAPPING: per block, ax = the 16-bit unsigned tap sample (din or n_delay_reg1..6) zero-extended to 18 bits; ay = that tap's constant coefficient zero-extended to 19 bits (largest is 255, well inside the 18/19-bit signed ranges); bx/by = the second tap/coefficient of the pair, same extension; result[27:0] is the pair's exact partial sum and feeds the soft adder tree into the existing dout flip-flop. Concretely, for the 7-term FIRs (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2 and h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg3, by=C3
U2: ax=n_delay_reg4, ay=C4, bx=n_delay_reg5, by=C5
U3: ax=n_delay_reg6, ay=C6, bx=19'd0 wired as bx=18'd0, by=19'd0 (unused term tied to 0; no port left unconnected)
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + (U2[27:0] + U3[27:0]);
For the 6-term FIRs (f3: 12,77,148,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,193,25,15; h3 and h4: 9,56,109,109,56,9 — the C3 tap is a literal 0 coefficient and has no term in the source):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=C4
U2: ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=C5, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=C6
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + U2[27:0];
PLAN:

What is converted. All 7 my_fir_* modules, each instantiated once inside h_fltr, which is instantiated 6 times (stereovision2.v:188-190, 195-197) — hence the 6 instances per region id. Per h_fltr: 4+4+4 blocks for the three 7-term FIRs (f1, f2, h2) and 3+3+3+3 for the four 6-term FIRs (f3, h1, h3, h4) = 24 blocks; ×6 instances = 144 blocks.

In f2 and h2 the two ×4 taps (lines 1542/1548 and 1716/1722) are not in the candidate list because synthesis currently implements them as shifts. They are terms of the same dout reduction, so they go onto block ports as coefficient 19'd4. That is exactly what the source writes; nothing is fabricated.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned; zero-extended to the 18-bit signed ax/bx they span 0..65535, inside the port's +131071 maximum (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). Coefficients are positive literals ≤ 255 on the 19-bit signed ay/by. Every product ≤ 65535·255 = 16 711 425; a block's two-product sum ≤ 33 422 850, exact in result[64]; the full 7-term sum ≤ 116 979 975 < 2^27, so the 28-bit dout and the 28-bit soft tree hold it without truncation, and the original 32-bit Verilog expression truncated to dout[27:0] loses nothing either. Integer addition is associative, so re-associating the 6 adds into a 2-level tree is bit-identical. No product is truncated or registered before the sum in these modules, so the "fused sum truncated after" hazard does not apply.

Live values and control preserved. dout keeps its own flip-flop and its if (new_data_rdy) enable — the blocks only replace the combinational expression driving it, so no register behaviour is deleted and module latency is unchanged (dout is still valid one cycle after the inputs, in step with output_data_ready). The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its enable, and output_data_ready are untouched. din and the delay registers stay real signals read by the shift chain; a block only takes a copy of an operand, so observability is preserved. Module ports and h_fltr's vidin_out_reg_* capture logic are unchanged; steer_fltr sees the same values.

Numbers required before committing.

  • Blocks: 144. Tiles: a comb_sop_2_18 takes its tile alone, so 144 tiles for the blocks. The 246 my_fir multiplies that currently sit two-to-a-tile disappear; the 252 fltr_compute 9×9 multiplies stay. Conservatively assuming the freed tiles are only those holding my_fir multiplies exclusively and no repacking of mixed tiles, the design lands at ≈226 tiles (+7 over the baseline 219); if the 9×9 multiplies repack four to a tile it is ≈207. Either way it is far inside the device's 770.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. The mode has no addend or chain port and a partial sum already needs 25 bits, so it cannot re-enter an 18-bit ax; partial sums are combined in soft logic. There is no block-to-block hop to charge.
  • Stage delay, median table values: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block-into-soft-logic wire 1.48 + two soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns, against the 12.4624 ns baseline critical path. Even taking the worst sampled wires on both hops (3.82 and 3.67) with median adders the stage is 11.85 ns, still below the 12.297 ns second path.
  • Soft logic leaving the touched stage: 4 of the 6 28-bit adder levels plus the final LUT in each 7-term FIR (3 of 5 levels in each 6-term FIR), i.e. roughly 110 carry-chain cells per FIR instance across 42 FIR instances. This is the slowest stage, and the second-slowest stage is the same shape in another my_fir instance — both are covered because every my_fir_* module and therefore every instance of that shape is converted. That is the reason the conversion is applied to all 7 modules rather than to the single reported critical path.
  • Expected ADP: ≈9.72e7 area (conservative 226 tiles, counting no cluster savings) × 8.09 ns ≈ 7.9e8, against the 1.18888e9 baseline — about 34% lower. Even if some untouched stage turns out to sit near 9.5 ns and caps the clock there, ADP is ≈9.2e8, still below baseline.

What is left, and why.

  • fltr_compute_f1 (ids 44-50), fltr_compute_f3 (72,73,74,76,77,78), fltr_compute_h4 (128-134) — 20 regions: the design registers each product in a 17-bit q and then sign-extends q[16] into the 20-bit sum (e.g. stereovision2.v:867). Their largest coefficients overflow that: 277·255=70635 (f1), 364·255=92820 (f3), 324·255=82620 (h4), all ≥ 2^16, so those products wrap negative before the sum. A fused block sums the untruncated products and produces a different value — "original truncates each product before the sum → no". Leave.
  • fltr_compute_f2 (59-63), fltr_compute_h1 (86-92), fltr_compute_h2 (101-105), fltr_compute_h3 (114-120) — 22 regions: these never exceed 65025, so fusion would be exact, but converting them cannot lower the clock. Their products are separated from the reduction by the q1..q7 pipeline registers (din→q→d_out_tmp→dout), so a combinational fusion would have to either delete a pipeline stage or re-introduce partial-sum registers, and in either case the three modules above stay unconverted and keep the identical 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage. A rewrite that leaves the governing stage of that class in place buys no period and is paid for in tiles alone (rule 1). Leave.

Risks. (1) The 2-level adder tree depends on the explicit parenthesisation ((U0+U1)+(U2+U3)) surviving synthesis; if the tool linearises it the stage grows by one adder level to ≈9.2 ns, still well under baseline. (2) Tile count is an estimate: the baseline packing of the 9×9 fltr_compute multiplies is not derivable from the source, so post-rewrite tiles could be anywhere in ≈207-226; the ADP win holds across that range. (3) If a stage outside my_fir — most plausibly the 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage in fltr_compute — is slower than 8.09 ns it becomes the new critical path and caps the gain; it cannot make the design slower than baseline, since no stage is lengthened. (4) No new operation or datatype family is introduced: the same integer products and the same integer sum, re-associated.

I'll verify the plan against the source.

APPROVE

Verified against /work/stereovision2.v:

  • Region/term accounting is real. The 41 listed $mul ids map exactly to the source terms: f1 7 terms (stereovision2.v:1486–1492), f2 5 mul-ids (:1543–:1547, with :1542/:1548 being the ×4 taps synthesized as shifts), f3 6 (:1602–:1607), h1 6 (:1661–:1666), h2 5 (:1717–:1721, ×4 taps at :1716/:1722), h3 6 (:1775–:1780), h4 6 (:1833–:1838) = 41. Coefficient lists in the plan match the defines (f1 29/101/15/235/15/101/29; f2,h2 4/42/163/255/163/42/4; f3 12/77/148/148/77/12; h1 15/25/193/193/25/15; h3,h4 9/56/109/109/56/9). The 6-term FIRs genuinely skip the n_delay_reg3 tap (COEF3 = 0, no term written), and the plan's pairing skips it too — no fabricated products (not (c)). Pulling the ×4 taps onto block ports is legitimate: they are written terms of the same dout sum, not invented ones.
  • Shared reduction is real. All terms in each module feed the single dout <= ... sum inside if (new_data_rdy) (e.g. :1486, :1716), so the terms genuinely share one reduction.
  • Function preserved (not (a)). Taps are 16-bit unsigned (WIDTH_5B=16), coefficients ≤ 255 positive, so all products are positive and zero-extension into signed 18/19-bit ports is exact. Worst-case full sum is f2/h2: 65535·673 = 44,105,055 < 2^27, so the 28-bit dout never truncated in the original either; re-associating into a 2-level tree is bit-identical. The dout flip-flop, its enable, the n_delay_reg shift chain and output_data_ready are untouched, so latency and control are unchanged.
  • No new op/datatype family (not (b)). Same integer multiply-accumulate on the same int32 reduction, mapped to the target comb_sop_2_18 mode.
  • No mode island (not (d)). Every block output feeds the existing dout adder tree → vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* (:479, :514–:520, :527) → steer_fltr (:521), a live consumer. The zero-padded second product in U3 of the 7-term FIRs is an unused port tied off on an otherwise live block, not a constant-driven block.
  • Instance count checks out. h_fltr instantiated 6× (:188–:190, :195–:197), one instance of each my_fir_* per h_fltr, giving 24 blocks × 6 = 144 and 246 converted multiplies.
  • The stated reason for leaving fltr_compute_f1/f3/h4 alone is also accurate: those products are registered into 17-bit q and sign-extended (:859–:867), and 277·255 = 70,635 ≥ 2^16 wraps negative, so fusing them would change the value.

Remaining items (tile-count range 207–226, whether the tool keeps the explicit parenthesisation, whether an untouched fltr_compute stage caps the clock) are estimates the plan itself flags as risks, and none lengthens a path relative to baseline — not grounds for revision.

REGIONS: my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1486$154, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1487$155, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1488$157, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1489$159, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1490$161, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1491$163, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1492$165, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1543$170, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1544$172, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1545$174, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1546$176, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1547$178, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1602$184, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1603$185, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1604$187, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1605$189, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1606$191, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1607$193, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1661$197, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1662$198, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1663$200, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1664$202, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1665$204, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1666$206, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1717$211, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1718$213, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1719$215, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1720$217, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1721$219, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1775$225, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1776$226, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1777$228, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1778$230, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1779$232, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1780$234, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1833$238, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1834$239, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1835$241, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1836$243, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1837$245, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1838$247
SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout sum in each my_fir_* module (stereovision2.v:1486, 1542, 1602, 1661, 1716, 1775, 1833) — the single 28-bit tap-sum registered into dout and driven out as h_fltr's vidin_out_temp_*
BLOCKS: 144
TILES: 226
STAGE_NS: 8.09
MAPPING: per block, ax = the 16-bit unsigned tap sample (din or n_delay_reg1..6) zero-extended to 18 bits; ay = that tap's constant coefficient zero-extended to 19 bits (largest is 255, well inside the 18/19-bit signed ranges); bx/by = the second tap/coefficient of the pair, same extension; result[27:0] is the pair's exact partial sum and feeds the soft adder tree into the existing dout flip-flop. Concretely, for the 7-term FIRs (f1: 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2 and h2: 4,42,163,255,163,42,4):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg3, by=C3
U2: ax=n_delay_reg4, ay=C4, bx=n_delay_reg5, by=C5
U3: ax=n_delay_reg6, ay=C6, bx=19'd0 wired as bx=18'd0, by=19'd0 (unused term tied to 0; no port left unconnected)
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + (U2[27:0] + U3[27:0]);
For the 6-term FIRs (f3: 12,77,148,148,77,12; h1: 15,25,193,193,25,15; h3 and h4: 9,56,109,109,56,9 — the C3 tap is a literal 0 coefficient and has no term in the source):
U0: ax=din, ay=C0, bx=n_delay_reg1, by=C1
U1: ax=n_delay_reg2, ay=C2, bx=n_delay_reg4, by=C4
U2: ax=n_delay_reg5, ay=C5, bx=n_delay_reg6, by=C6
dout <= (U0[27:0] + U1[27:0]) + U2[27:0];
PLAN:

What is converted. All 7 my_fir_* modules, each instantiated once inside h_fltr, which is instantiated 6 times (stereovision2.v:188-190, 195-197) — hence the 6 instances per region id. Per h_fltr: 4+4+4 blocks for the three 7-term FIRs (f1, f2, h2) and 3+3+3+3 for the four 6-term FIRs (f3, h1, h3, h4) = 24 blocks; ×6 instances = 144 blocks.

In f2 and h2 the two ×4 taps (lines 1542/1548 and 1716/1722) are not in the candidate list because synthesis currently implements them as shifts. They are terms of the same dout reduction, so they go onto block ports as coefficient 19'd4. That is exactly what the source writes; nothing is fabricated.

Exactness. din and n_delay_reg1..6 are 16-bit unsigned; zero-extended to the 18-bit signed ax/bx they span 0..65535, inside the port's +131071 maximum (unsigned operand narrower than the port → zero-extend, allowed). Coefficients are positive literals ≤ 255 on the 19-bit signed ay/by. Every product ≤ 65535·255 = 16 711 425; a block's two-product sum ≤ 33 422 850, exact in result[64]; the full 7-term sum ≤ 116 979 975 < 2^27, so the 28-bit dout and the 28-bit soft tree hold it without truncation, and the original 32-bit Verilog expression truncated to dout[27:0] loses nothing either. Integer addition is associative, so re-associating the 6 adds into a 2-level tree is bit-identical. No product is truncated or registered before the sum in these modules, so the "fused sum truncated after" hazard does not apply.

Live values and control preserved. dout keeps its own flip-flop and its if (new_data_rdy) enable — the blocks only replace the combinational expression driving it, so no register behaviour is deleted and module latency is unchanged (dout is still valid one cycle after the inputs, in step with output_data_ready). The n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, its enable, and output_data_ready are untouched. din and the delay registers stay real signals read by the shift chain; a block only takes a copy of an operand, so observability is preserved. Module ports and h_fltr's vidin_out_reg_* capture logic are unchanged; steer_fltr sees the same values.

Numbers required before committing.

  • Blocks: 144. Tiles: a comb_sop_2_18 takes its tile alone, so 144 tiles for the blocks. The 246 my_fir multiplies that currently sit two-to-a-tile disappear; the 252 fltr_compute 9×9 multiplies stay. Conservatively assuming the freed tiles are only those holding my_fir multiplies exclusively and no repacking of mixed tiles, the design lands at ≈226 tiles (+7 over the baseline 219); if the 9×9 multiplies repack four to a tile it is ≈207. Either way it is far inside the device's 770.
  • Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1. The mode has no addend or chain port and a partial sum already needs 25 bits, so it cannot re-enter an 18-bit ax; partial sums are combined in soft logic. There is no block-to-block hop to charge.
  • Stage delay, median table values: clock-to-Q 0.06 + register-to-first-block wire 2.25 + block 2.14 + block-into-soft-logic wire 1.48 + two soft adder levels 2×1.08 = 8.09 ns, against the 12.4624 ns baseline critical path. Even taking the worst sampled wires on both hops (3.82 and 3.67) with median adders the stage is 11.85 ns, still below the 12.297 ns second path.
  • Soft logic leaving the touched stage: 4 of the 6 28-bit adder levels plus the final LUT in each 7-term FIR (3 of 5 levels in each 6-term FIR), i.e. roughly 110 carry-chain cells per FIR instance across 42 FIR instances. This is the slowest stage, and the second-slowest stage is the same shape in another my_fir instance — both are covered because every my_fir_* module and therefore every instance of that shape is converted. That is the reason the conversion is applied to all 7 modules rather than to the single reported critical path.
  • Expected ADP: ≈9.72e7 area (conservative 226 tiles, counting no cluster savings) × 8.09 ns ≈ 7.9e8, against the 1.18888e9 baseline — about 34% lower. Even if some untouched stage turns out to sit near 9.5 ns and caps the clock there, ADP is ≈9.2e8, still below baseline.

What is left, and why.

  • fltr_compute_f1 (ids 44-50), fltr_compute_f3 (72,73,74,76,77,78), fltr_compute_h4 (128-134) — 20 regions: the design registers each product in a 17-bit q and then sign-extends q[16] into the 20-bit sum (e.g. stereovision2.v:867). Their largest coefficients overflow that: 277·255=70635 (f1), 364·255=92820 (f3), 324·255=82620 (h4), all ≥ 2^16, so those products wrap negative before the sum. A fused block sums the untruncated products and produces a different value — "original truncates each product before the sum → no". Leave.
  • fltr_compute_f2 (59-63), fltr_compute_h1 (86-92), fltr_compute_h2 (101-105), fltr_compute_h3 (114-120) — 22 regions: these never exceed 65025, so fusion would be exact, but converting them cannot lower the clock. Their products are separated from the reduction by the q1..q7 pipeline registers (din→q→d_out_tmp→dout), so a combinational fusion would have to either delete a pipeline stage or re-introduce partial-sum registers, and in either case the three modules above stay unconverted and keep the identical 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage. A rewrite that leaves the governing stage of that class in place buys no period and is paid for in tiles alone (rule 1). Leave.

Risks. (1) The 2-level adder tree depends on the explicit parenthesisation ((U0+U1)+(U2+U3)) surviving synthesis; if the tool linearises it the stage grows by one adder level to ≈9.2 ns, still well under baseline. (2) Tile count is an estimate: the baseline packing of the 9×9 fltr_compute multiplies is not derivable from the source, so post-rewrite tiles could be anywhere in ≈207-226; the ADP win holds across that range. (3) If a stage outside my_fir — most plausibly the 6-adder-level d_out_tmp stage in fltr_compute — is slower than 8.09 ns it becomes the new critical path and caps the gain; it cannot make the design slower than baseline, since no stage is lengthened. (4) No new operation or datatype family is introduced: the same integer products and the same integer sum, re-associated.

Do NOT run place-and-route or any VTR/vpr command — that is verified separately. Stop when check.sh reports it synthesizes cleanly and its cell census shows the intended arithmetic. Edited /work/stereovision2.v — the 7 my_fir_* modules only; nothing else in the design touched.

In each module the dout tap-sum expression was re-expressed as a fused sum-of-products on comb_sop_2_18 hard blocks feeding a shallow soft adder tree:

  • 7-tap FIRs (my_fir_f1 :1486, my_fir_f2 :1542, my_fir_h2 :1716) — 4 blocks each: (din,C0)+(reg1,C1), (reg2,C2)+(reg3,C3), (reg4,C4)+(reg5,C5), (reg6,C6)+(0,0) with the unused term tied off as bx=18'd0, by=19'd0. Sum: ((U0+U1)+(U2+U3)).
  • 6-tap FIRs (my_fir_f3 :1602, my_fir_h1 :1661, my_fir_h3 :1775, my_fir_h4 :1833 — the C3 tap has no term in the source) — 3 blocks each: (din,C0)+(reg1,C1), (reg2,C2)+(reg4,C4), (reg5,C5)+(reg6,C6). Sum: ((U0+U1)+U2).

Operand widening follows the block's signed convention: the 16-bit unsigned samples are zero-extended to the 18-bit ax/bx ({2'b00, ...}), and the positive constant coefficients (≤255) are literals on the 19-bit ay/by. result[27:0] is exact (max 7-term sum 116,979,975 < 2²⁷) and drives the existing 28-bit dout flip-flop, which keeps its own if (new_data_rdy) enable; the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain, output_data_ready, module ports, and h_fltr/steer_fltr consumers are unchanged. All four input ports are driven on every instance.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: syntax + elaboration + cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Runs plain yosys (read_verilog; hierarchy; proc; opt; stat) on the one file: no arch, no
# parmys, no hard-block library. Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole
# design (the last "Number of cells" table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub,
# registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated
# module by name, which is where a hard-block instantiation such as comb_mult_add_16 shows up
# with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
out="$(yosys -p "read_verilog $f; hierarchy -auto-top; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt

You are an INDEPENDENT ACCEPTANCE REVIEWER — the gate of record for whether an adapted design is
a LEGITIMATE member of its benchmark suite. You did NOT plan or write this edit. Judge only what
is in front of you. Your verdict is final and fully automated; there is no human backstop, so err
toward rejecting anything you cannot positively ground.

FRAMING — refute by default. Your job is to find why this adaptation is NOT a legitimate suite
member. Assume it is contrived until the evidence shows otherwise. A rewrite that merely "makes
the mode appear in the netlist" is NOT sufficient — the mode must do real work the original
workload actually implied.

This case has already passed the deterministic structural, provenance, and (for T3) evidence
gates — do not re-litigate those mechanically. Your judgement is the legitimacy call they
cannot make: would a real designer of THIS workload ship this, or is it a mode-island / fabricated
dot-product / gratuitous variant dressed up to pass?

REJECT if any of these hold:

  • (a) FABRICATED TERMS — a fused N-term product/sum aggregates operands that did not genuinely
    share a reduction in the original; the "dot product" is manufactured to fill the mode's arity.
  • (b) MODE ISLAND / DEAD WORK — the mode's result does not flow to the design's real outputs, or
    is wired to constants / a sink that the rest of the design ignores.
  • (c) ROLE BREAK — the adapted region no longer plays the workload role the witness recorded
    (e.g. a control-path multiply repurposed as a fake datapath MAC).
  • (d) UNGROUNDED VARIANT (T3) — the precision/algorithm variant is not the one the cited sibling
    design evidences; the adaptation drifts to a workload character the suite does not contain.

ACCEPT if the adaptation is sound: the mapped arithmetic genuinely existed, the fused terms really
shared a reduction, the mode's output drives the original consumers, and the workload role is
preserved. Legitimate adaptation need not be optimal — only real.

You MUST ground your verdict: cite the specific preserved role (from the witness), the real
region(s)/reduction the mode maps onto, and — for T3 — the sibling evidence axis. A verdict you
cannot ground is a REJECT.

response

I'll examine the file before rendering a verdict.

ACCEPT

Role preserved (witness). The witness records role my_fir_f1…h4, operation sop, with the shared reduction being "the single 28-bit tap-sum registered into dout" in each my_fir_* module. Post-edit, each of the seven modules is still a 7-tap transposed-input FIR: the n_delay_reg1..6 shift chain is untouched (stereovision2.v:1504-1509, 1954-1959), output_data_ready still pulses with new_data_rdy, and dout is still the 28-bit registered tap-sum (stereovision2.v:1512, 1663, 1962). The workload character — a video-rate steerable-filter FIR bank — is unchanged.

Real region mapped, no fabricated terms. Every fused pair aggregates two products that were literally adjacent addends of the same dout expression in the pristine source. E.g. my_fir_f1: fir_sop_01 = din*COEF0_b + n_delay_reg1*COEF1_b (pristine lines 1486-1487 of the witness region list), fir_sop_23/fir_sop_45 likewise for taps 2/3 and 4/5. No operand is imported from another module, another filter, or another reduction; no cross-module fusion occurs. The 6-tap modules (f3, h1, h3, h4, whose COEF3_* is 0 and whose pristine dout skipped n_delay_reg3) pair (0,1),(2,4),(5,6) — exactly the six terms that were present, and n_delay_reg3 is still correctly absent (stereovision2.v:1636-1649). In the 7-tap modules the odd seventh tap occupies ax/ay of fir_sop_6x with the unused bx/by slot tied to zero; that is arity padding of a 2-term block, not an invented addend.

Not a mode island. tap_sum → dout → vidin_out_temp_* → vidin_out_reg_* (stereovision2.v:514-520, 527-553) → steer_fltr (:521) → real_z/imag_z/real_p/imag_p/real_n/imag_n registers, which are h_fltr's outputs. The mode's results drive the original consumers listed in the manifest; nothing is sunk.

Numerically faithful. WIDTH_5B = 16, so the 16-bit unsigned samples zero-extend cleanly into the 18-bit signed ax/bx, and all active coefficients are positive and ≤ 255 (stereovision2.v:1440-1906), well inside 19-bit signed. Taking result[27:0] and summing mod 2^28 reproduces the pristine 28-bit truncated dout bit-for-bit (worst-case true sum ≈ 4.4e7 < 2^28 anyway).

T3 evidence axis: not applicable — T2, manifest introduces no operations or datatypes, evidence floor waived.

No (a), (b), (c), or (d) violation found. A designer targeting a 2-term 18×19 SOP hard block in this FIR bank would write substantially this.

1631		// the 16-bit unsigned samples are zero-extended to the 18-bit signed ax/bx,

1647			.result(sop_56));

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision2 under this run's VPR settings (regime 'koios_medium': channel width 300, device koios_medium, seed 1) has a critical path of 12.4624 ns and uses 219 DSP tiles of the device's 770, one block per tile. Its used logic-block area is 9.53973e+07 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905), so its area-delay product is 1.18888e+09. Its slowest stage is in my_fir: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19), then six adder levels with their carry chains, then one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop. The next slowest stage, in a different my_fir instance (endpoint group $dffe~788, not path 1's $dffe~456), is the same shape as path 1 — one hard multiply on a DSP tile, six adder levels with their carry chains, one LUT, into an enabled flip-flop, 12.297 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Rewrite rules

A stage is the logic between one register and the next. A block is one instance of the target mode. These rules hold for any synchronous design on any FPGA. Numbers that depend on this architecture are in the last section; use them, do not restate them as rules.

Must hold

Going against these produces a wrong design, not a slower one.

Observability. Only a block's output is visible. A value read by anything other than the arithmetic being taken into the block (another expression, a module output port, another module) cannot go inside a block. Keep it outside, or recompute it for those readers. Check the source; detection does not see port consumers. Operands are exempt: the block takes a copy and the original signal still exists.

Register control. A register carries its reset, enable and initial value. These modes hold no registers, so taking a register inside deletes that behaviour. If anything depends on it, keep the register and drive it from the block's result, or leave the site alone.

Arithmetic. The block computes the mode's expression on signed ports. Match the original bit for bit or leave the site alone.

design operandon a signed portallowed
unsigned, narrower than the portzero-extendyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range provably below the signed maximum (constant coefficient, bounded value)zero-extend, state the bound and where it comes fromyes
unsigned, as wide as the port, range not boundable from the source (a top-level input)no exact representationno
original truncates each product before the suma fused sum truncated after differsno
original's narrow sum register wrapsthe low bits of the wider result reproduce the wrapyes
Weigh

The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage in the whole design. A stage's delay is the sum of everything on it: each block's fixed delay, each wire, each soft cell. Each relationship below names what it does to delay and to area. If a plan goes against one, say why.

1. Slowest stage → all outputs

Your task names the design's slowest stage and what is on it.

Slowest stage → delay

Relationship: A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage cannot make the design faster, and must leave every stage no slower than it was. A rewrite that does touch it must make it shorter, not merely different. Compare every stage you change against it.

Slowest stage → area

Relationship: A rewrite that cannot shorten the clock period is paid for in tiles alone. Say so in the plan.

2. Block to register → delay

Relationship: A block's result goes to a register, or into the next block, with nothing but wire between. Any soft arithmetic between a block and its register is added to the stage.

3. Blocks in series → all outputs
Blocks in series → delay

Relationship: Each block in series adds its own delay plus one wire. Sum them with the wire in and the wire out, and compare against the slowest stage. That comparison, not a fixed count, says how many fit.

Blocks in series → area

Relationship: None. Series or side by side, the same blocks take the same tiles.

4. Arithmetic inside the block → all outputs

Arithmetic inside the block is what the mode's expression computes rather than what sits in series with it: a multiply and one addend, or two products and their sum, depending on the mode.

Arithmetic inside the block → delay

Relationship: Free. The block's delay is the mode's own and does not rise because its adder is used. This is where a conversion wins: soft logic leaves the stage and the block's delay already covers the work. An adder feeding the block's addend port is mapped into the block; an adder reading the block's result is on the path after it (rule 2). A block whose addend port is tied to a constant takes in nothing; if the multiply it replaces is already on a tile, the conversion changes the tile count and nothing else.

Arithmetic inside the block → area

Relationship: Flip-flops fall where product registers go. Do not count on LUT savings.

5. Registers → all outputs
Registers → delay

Relationship: A register may move only if no stage becomes longer and the module's latency is unchanged. If one must be added to keep latency, put it where it splits the longest stage. Every other operand entering that stage must be delayed by the same amount, or the stage adds values from different cycles.

Registers → area

Relationship: A register placed mid-chain costs operand-delay registers that a register after the chain does not.

6. Tiles → area

Relationship: A block occupies one DSP tile in one mode. Do not assume the multiply it replaces frees a tile: the synthesis tool may already have placed that multiply on a tile shared with others. Count the blocks against the tiles the unmodified design uses and the device's count, both in your task.

Measured on this architecture

Observed across 12 timing reports through 2026-09-20; block, clock-to-Q and packing rows are from the architecture file and exact; wire and adder rows are sampled ranges with the median in parentheses.

Every DSP mode costs the same 2.14 ns. The invented modes have no chain ports, so a partial sum passes between blocks through general routing. The packer can place up to four multiplies of 9 bits or less on one tile and up to two of 18 bits or less; a block in either invented mode takes its tile alone.

Before you commit to a plan

State in your plan: how many blocks and how many tiles on top of the unmodified design's; the longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay from the table, against the slowest stage; and what soft logic leaves the stage you touch, and whether that stage is the slowest one. If a site does not fit under those numbers, arrange it differently, take fewer, or leave it, and name the rule that ruled it out.

Objective: minimize the design's area-delay product, VPR's used area times the critical path, against the baseline in your task. A rewrite that does not lower it is not worth making; say so and output TIER: NONE rather than convert sites for their own sake. Among rewrites that lower it, prefer the one that lowers it most.

Classify the tier of the change you propose (a datatype/algorithm variant, T3, is out of scope —
do not propose one):

  • T1: Direct replacement: no change to the computed function or surrounding structure; no datatype change.
  • T2: Local restructuring only; preserve the region's function, role, and live consumers; no new operation/datatype family.

Read the source. Output EXACTLY this header (uppercase keys), then the plan:

TIER: T1 | T2 | NONE
REGIONS: <comma-separated region id(s)> # one id for a direct swap; N ids for a fusion
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal/module the terms feed> # or NONE for a single region
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, a single integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, a single integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage your rewrite leaves, in ns, a single number>
MAPPING: <which operands map to which of the mode's ports>
PLAN: <how the module's live inputs/outputs and downstream consumers are preserved; any introduced
operations/datatypes (should be NONE for T1/T2); and the main risks>

Output rules: no preamble before the TIER line — read the source first, then write. You
are one step of an automated pipeline: a reviewer runs automatically on this plan, you
cannot invoke it, and no human reads your output, so do not end with a question or an
offer.

This is not a failure; it is a request to do better.

SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout <= tap-sum in each my_fir_* instance (all listed products already feed that one accumulator; the rewrite splits it into a registered partial s/pre-sum registers and the final dout adder, all still the same single reduction)
BLOCKS: 90
TILES: 146
STAGE_NS: 8.1
MAPPING:

  • 6-tap modules (my_fir_f3 C=12,77,148; my_fir_h1 C=15,25,193; my_fir_h3/h4 C=9,56,109), 2 blocks per instance:

    • B0: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd C0, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg6}, by=19'd C0 → C0·x[n] + C0·x[n-6]
    • B2: ax={1'b0,p15}, ay=19'd C1, bx={1'b0,p24}, by=19'd C2 → C1(x[n-1]+x[n-5]) + C2(x[n-2]+x[n-4])
    • dout <= B0.result[27:0] + B2.result[27:0];

  • my_fir_f1 (29,101,15,235), 3 blocks per instance:

    • BS1: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd101, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg4}, by=19'd101
    • BS2: ax={1'b0,u}, ay=19'd15, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg2}, by=19'd235
    • B0: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd29, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg6}, by=19'd29
    • s <= BS1.result[27:0] + BS2.result[27:0]; dout <= B0.result[27:0] + s;

  • my_fir_f2 / my_fir_h2 (4,42,163,255), 2 blocks per instance (the ×4 taps stay the shift the baseline already uses — they are not candidates):

    • BS1: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd42, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg4}, by=19'd42
    • BS2: ax={1'b0,u}, ay=19'd163, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg2}, by=19'd255
    • s <= BS1.result[27:0] + BS2.result[27:0]; dout <= {9'b0, (din + n_delay_reg6), 2'b00} + s;
  • No block has an unused term port; every ax/ay/bx/by is driven.

PLAN:

What changes. All 41 my_fir multiply candidates move onto comb_sop_2_18 blocks, but with the tap sum retimed so that each stage holds at most one block followed by at most one soft adder. Two devices do the work:

  1. Pre-sum registers. The filters are symmetric (f1 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). Equal-coefficient tap pairs are added before the multiply, but the add is done one enabled cycle early and registered, so it costs no delay on the multiply stage:

    • p15 <= din + n_delay_reg4; holds x[n-1]+x[n-5] at cycle n.
    • p24 <= n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg3; holds x[n-2]+x[n-4] at cycle n.
    • u <= din + n_delay_reg2; holds x[n-1]+x[n-3] at cycle n, i.e. x[n-2]+x[n-4] at cycle n-1 (used by the 7-tap partial).
      The pair that involves the current sample, C0·x[n] + C0·x[n-6], is not pre-added — it is given the block's two product slots instead, so no combinational pre-adder ever lands on the multiply stage.
  2. Registered partial s (7-tap modules f1, f2, h2 only). s <= 101·din + 101·reg4 + 15·u + 235·reg2 (f1) is loaded at cycle n-1 and therefore equals C1(x[n-1]+x[n-5]) + C2(x[n-2]+x[n-4]) + C3·x[n-3] at cycle n. That leaves the final stage with one block plus one adder.

Exactness. Every product term the original computes is still computed with the same operand and the same constant; only associativity and the cycle in which sub-sums are formed change (distributivity C·a + C·b = C·(a+b) on pairs, plus retiming). din and the delay registers are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended into the 18-bit signed ax/bx (narrower than the port); p15/p24/u are 17-bit unsigned sums ≤ 131070 < 2^17, zero-extended into the 18-bit signed port; every coefficient is a positive constant ≤ 255 in the 19-bit signed ay/by. No term is negative and no intermediate is truncated before the sum: the largest full sum is f2/h2 at 65535·673 = 4.41e7, well under 2^28, so the 28-bit s and dout registers wrap exactly as the original (which also cannot overflow 28 bits) — the baseline's 32-bit expression truncated to 28 bits is bit-identical.

Latency, control, observability. dout[n] remains the same function of x[n]…x[n-6], so module latency and the output_data_ready protocol are unchanged; the new registers p15, p24, u, s live inside the same if (new_data_rdy) branch, so they advance exactly when the delay line advances and hold when it holds — the retiming is valid over the enabled-cycle sequence. The deepest history any new path reads is still x[n-6], so the warm-up behaviour after reset is the same as the baseline's (both undefined until the delay line fills; neither has a reset or initial value). dout keeps its own enabled register driven by the final adder. n_delay_reg1..6 are read only inside the module, so feeding them to the pre-sum adders and block ports is safe; module ports din, dout, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready are untouched, so sv_chip2_hierarchy_no_mem needs no change. No new operation or datatype family is introduced (adds, multiplies and registers only).

Numbers I commit to. 90 blocks: 3 per my_fir_f1 instance, 2 per instance of the other six my_fir modules, ×6 instances each = (3+2+2+2+2+2+2)·6. Blocks take their tile alone, and after the rewrite no my_fir multiply survives outside a block, so total tiles = 90 + the 56 tiles the fltr_compute 9×9 multiplies occupy (200 measured − 144 blocks) = 146, down 54 from the measured 200 and 73 below the baseline's 219. That is 54·253779 ≈ 1.37e7 of tile area removed; against it I add roughly 66 CLBs of new registers and enable muxes (p15/p24/u 17-bit, s 28-bit) net of the adder level each module loses, ≈ +0.18e7, for an expected used-logic area near 7.3e7 (vs 8.54e7 measured).

Longest block series and the stage I leave. No block ever feeds another block: the longest series in any stage is one block, 2.14 ns. The slowest stage the rewrite leaves is the final dout stage of f1/f3/h1/h3/h4: clock-to-Q 0.06–0.10 + register-to-block wire ≈1.31 + block 2.14 + block-to-adder wire ≈1.38 + one 28-bit carry chain ≈0.75 + wire into the dffe enable-mux LUT ≈2.23 + LUT 0.15 ≈ 8.1 ns. The s stage of f1/f2/h2 is the same shape (block + one adder + enable mux) ≈8.1 ns; the f2/h2 final stage (shift-add + final add, no block) ≈5.8 ns; the pre-sum register stages ≈3.5 ns. What leaves the current critical stage is one full soft adder level (wire 0.79 + carry 0.56) and the seven-term tree's second level entirely — the measured path's $add~206→$add~207 pair collapses to a single adder. This stage is the design's slowest stage (the measured 8.71 ns path is exactly this my_fir dout stage), so the delay saving is real rather than paid for in tiles; the tile saving is on top of it. Expected area×delay ≈ 7.3e7 × 8.1 ≈ 6.0e8, versus 7.44e8 for the current rewrite and 1.189e9 for the baseline. Even if routing gives back the whole delay gain and the period stays at 8.71 ns, the area reduction alone puts the product at ≈6.4e8, still below the current result.

Candidates left, with reasons.

  • All 42 fltr_compute candidates (fltr_compute_f1 $44–$50, f2 $59–$63, f3 $72–$78, h1 $86–$92, h2 $101–$105, h3 $114–$120, h4 $128–$134): left for two independent reasons. (a) Arithmetic: each product is stored in a 17-bit register q1..q7 and then sign-extended as a signed 17-bit value into the 20-bit sum ({q[16],q[16],q[16],q}). Coefficients such as 9'b100010101 (277) give 255·277 = 70,635, which sets bit 16, so the baseline sum deliberately reinterprets that product as negative. A fused ax*ay+bx*by sums the true products and differs by 2^17, and dout takes bits [18:3] of that sum, so the difference is visible — the "original truncates each product before the sum" row forbids the fusion. (b) Even for the pairs whose products can never set bit 16, these multiplies are 8×9 and pack four to a DSP tile today (44 of 54 tiles); one comb_sop_2_18 block covers only two products and takes a tile alone, so converting them raises the tile count, and their stages (the registered multiply stage and the separate q-sum stage) are not the design's slowest stage, so rule 1 says the extra tiles would buy no period. Leaving them untouched also keeps the 56-tile figure above exact.

Risks. (1) The retiming is the main correctness risk: p15/p24/u/s must be loaded from the taps one enabled cycle early exactly as written above — an off-by-one in which delay register feeds a pre-sum silently shifts a tap. The implementer should re-derive each register's content at cycle n from its load expression at cycle n−1 before committing. (2) All new registers must sit inside the if (new_data_rdy) branch; putting any of them outside it breaks equivalence whenever new_data_rdy is low. (3) The 8.1 ns figure assumes median routing for the register→block and adder→enable-mux hops; those two wires are ~3.5 ns of the stage and are not under the rewrite's control, so the realized period may land between 8.1 and 8.7 ns — the tile reduction keeps the product below the current result across that range. (4) For f2/h2 the ×4 taps must stay a shift: write them as the explicit concatenation {9'b0, (din + n_delay_reg6), 2'b00} rather than *4, so synthesis cannot re-infer a multiplier and claim another tile.

The objective is the area-delay product: VPR's "Total used logic block area" (minimum-width transistor areas, I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path. Area and delay are each taken as the median over the seed list, independently, so the recorded pair need not come from one placement; every seed's raw numbers are in the table below. In search mode the step fires whenever there is a product to improve on, whether or not it beat the baseline: a result better than the baseline is not the same as the best available.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1486$154, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1487$155, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1488$157, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1489$159, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1490$161, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1491$163, my_fir_f1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1492$165, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1543$170, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1544$172, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1545$174, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1546$176, my_fir_f2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1547$178, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1602$184, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1603$185, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1604$187, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1605$189, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1606$191, my_fir_f3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1607$193, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1661$197, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1662$198, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1663$200, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1664$202, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1665$204, my_fir_h1.$mul$stereovision2.v:1666$206, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1717$211, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1718$213, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1719$215, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1720$217, my_fir_h2.$mul$stereovision2.v:1721$219, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1775$225, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1776$226, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1777$228, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1778$230, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1779$232, my_fir_h3.$mul$stereovision2.v:1780$234, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1833$238, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1834$239, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1835$241, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1836$243, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1837$245, my_fir_h4.$mul$stereovision2.v:1838$247
SHARED_REDUCTION: the dout <= tap-sum in each my_fir_* instance (all listed products already feed that one accumulator; the rewrite splits it into a registered partial s/pre-sum registers and the final dout adder, all still the same single reduction)
BLOCKS: 90
TILES: 146
STAGE_NS: 8.1
MAPPING:

  • 6-tap modules (my_fir_f3 C=12,77,148; my_fir_h1 C=15,25,193; my_fir_h3/h4 C=9,56,109), 2 blocks per instance:

    • B0: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd C0, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg6}, by=19'd C0 → C0·x[n] + C0·x[n-6]
    • B2: ax={1'b0,p15}, ay=19'd C1, bx={1'b0,p24}, by=19'd C2 → C1(x[n-1]+x[n-5]) + C2(x[n-2]+x[n-4])
    • dout <= B0.result[27:0] + B2.result[27:0];

  • my_fir_f1 (29,101,15,235), 3 blocks per instance:

    • BS1: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd101, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg4}, by=19'd101
    • BS2: ax={1'b0,u}, ay=19'd15, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg2}, by=19'd235
    • B0: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd29, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg6}, by=19'd29
    • s <= BS1.result[27:0] + BS2.result[27:0]; dout <= B0.result[27:0] + s;

  • my_fir_f2 / my_fir_h2 (4,42,163,255), 2 blocks per instance (the ×4 taps stay the shift the baseline already uses — they are not candidates):

    • BS1: ax={2'b0,din}, ay=19'd42, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg4}, by=19'd42
    • BS2: ax={1'b0,u}, ay=19'd163, bx={2'b0,n_delay_reg2}, by=19'd255
    • s <= BS1.result[27:0] + BS2.result[27:0]; dout <= {9'b0, (din + n_delay_reg6), 2'b00} + s;
  • No block has an unused term port; every ax/ay/bx/by is driven.

PLAN:

What changes. All 41 my_fir multiply candidates move onto comb_sop_2_18 blocks, but with the tap sum retimed so that each stage holds at most one block followed by at most one soft adder. Two devices do the work:

  1. Pre-sum registers. The filters are symmetric (f1 29,101,15,235,15,101,29; f2/h2 4,42,163,255,163,42,4; f3 12,77,148,0,148,77,12; h1 15,25,193,0,193,25,15; h3/h4 9,56,109,0,109,56,9). Equal-coefficient tap pairs are added before the multiply, but the add is done one enabled cycle early and registered, so it costs no delay on the multiply stage:

    • p15 <= din + n_delay_reg4; holds x[n-1]+x[n-5] at cycle n.
    • p24 <= n_delay_reg1 + n_delay_reg3; holds x[n-2]+x[n-4] at cycle n.
    • u <= din + n_delay_reg2; holds x[n-1]+x[n-3] at cycle n, i.e. x[n-2]+x[n-4] at cycle n-1 (used by the 7-tap partial).
      The pair that involves the current sample, C0·x[n] + C0·x[n-6], is not pre-added — it is given the block's two product slots instead, so no combinational pre-adder ever lands on the multiply stage.
  2. Registered partial s (7-tap modules f1, f2, h2 only). s <= 101·din + 101·reg4 + 15·u + 235·reg2 (f1) is loaded at cycle n-1 and therefore equals C1(x[n-1]+x[n-5]) + C2(x[n-2]+x[n-4]) + C3·x[n-3] at cycle n. That leaves the final stage with one block plus one adder.

Exactness. Every product term the original computes is still computed with the same operand and the same constant; only associativity and the cycle in which sub-sums are formed change (distributivity C·a + C·b = C·(a+b) on pairs, plus retiming). din and the delay registers are 16-bit unsigned, zero-extended into the 18-bit signed ax/bx (narrower than the port); p15/p24/u are 17-bit unsigned sums ≤ 131070 < 2^17, zero-extended into the 18-bit signed port; every coefficient is a positive constant ≤ 255 in the 19-bit signed ay/by. No term is negative and no intermediate is truncated before the sum: the largest full sum is f2/h2 at 65535·673 = 4.41e7, well under 2^28, so the 28-bit s and dout registers wrap exactly as the original (which also cannot overflow 28 bits) — the baseline's 32-bit expression truncated to 28 bits is bit-identical.

Latency, control, observability. dout[n] remains the same function of x[n]…x[n-6], so module latency and the output_data_ready protocol are unchanged; the new registers p15, p24, u, s live inside the same if (new_data_rdy) branch, so they advance exactly when the delay line advances and hold when it holds — the retiming is valid over the enabled-cycle sequence. The deepest history any new path reads is still x[n-6], so the warm-up behaviour after reset is the same as the baseline's (both undefined until the delay line fills; neither has a reset or initial value). dout keeps its own enabled register driven by the final adder. n_delay_reg1..6 are read only inside the module, so feeding them to the pre-sum adders and block ports is safe; module ports din, dout, new_data_rdy, output_data_ready are untouched, so sv_chip2_hierarchy_no_mem needs no change. No new operation or datatype family is introduced (adds, multiplies and registers only).

Numbers I commit to. 90 blocks: 3 per my_fir_f1 instance, 2 per instance of the other six my_fir modules, ×6 instances each = (3+2+2+2+2+2+2)·6. Blocks take their tile alone, and after the rewrite no my_fir multiply survives outside a block, so total tiles = 90 + the 56 tiles the fltr_compute 9×9 multiplies occupy (200 measured − 144 blocks) = 146, down 54 from the measured 200 and 73 below the baseline's 219. That is 54·253779 ≈ 1.37e7 of tile area removed; against it I add roughly 66 CLBs of new registers and enable muxes (p15/p24/u 17-bit, s 28-bit) net of the adder level each module loses, ≈ +0.18e7, for an expected used-logic area near 7.3e7 (vs 8.54e7 measured).

Longest block series and the stage I leave. No block ever feeds another block: the longest series in any stage is one block, 2.14 ns. The slowest stage the rewrite leaves is the final dout stage of f1/f3/h1/h3/h4: clock-to-Q 0.06–0.10 + register-to-block wire ≈1.31 + block 2.14 + block-to-adder wire ≈1.38 + one 28-bit carry chain ≈0.75 + wire into the dffe enable-mux LUT ≈2.23 + LUT 0.15 ≈ 8.1 ns. The s stage of f1/f2/h2 is the same shape (block + one adder + enable mux) ≈8.1 ns; the f2/h2 final stage (shift-add + final add, no block) ≈5.8 ns; the pre-sum register stages ≈3.5 ns. What leaves the current critical stage is one full soft adder level (wire 0.79 + carry 0.56) and the seven-term tree's second level entirely — the measured path's $add~206→$add~207 pair collapses to a single adder. This stage is the design's slowest stage (the measured 8.71 ns path is exactly this my_fir dout stage), so the delay saving is real rather than paid for in tiles; the tile saving is on top of it. Expected area×delay ≈ 7.3e7 × 8.1 ≈ 6.0e8, versus 7.44e8 for the current rewrite and 1.189e9 for the baseline. Even if routing gives back the whole delay gain and the period stays at 8.71 ns, the area reduction alone puts the product at ≈6.4e8, still below the current result.

Candidates left, with reasons.

  • All 42 fltr_compute candidates (fltr_compute_f1 $44–$50, f2 $59–$63, f3 $72–$78, h1 $86–$92, h2 $101–$105, h3 $114–$120, h4 $128–$134): left for two independent reasons. (a) Arithmetic: each product is stored in a 17-bit register q1..q7 and then sign-extended as a signed 17-bit value into the 20-bit sum ({q[16],q[16],q[16],q}). Coefficients such as 9'b100010101 (277) give 255·277 = 70,635, which sets bit 16, so the baseline sum deliberately reinterprets that product as negative. A fused ax*ay+bx*by sums the true products and differs by 2^17, and dout takes bits [18:3] of that sum, so the difference is visible — the "original truncates each product before the sum" row forbids the fusion. (b) Even for the pairs whose products can never set bit 16, these multiplies are 8×9 and pack four to a DSP tile today (44 of 54 tiles); one comb_sop_2_18 block covers only two products and takes a tile alone, so converting them raises the tile count, and their stages (the registered multiply stage and the separate q-sum stage) are not the design's slowest stage, so rule 1 says the extra tiles would buy no period. Leaving them untouched also keeps the 56-tile figure above exact.

Risks. (1) The retiming is the main correctness risk: p15/p24/u/s must be loaded from the taps one enabled cycle early exactly as written above — an off-by-one in which delay register feeds a pre-sum silently shifts a tap. The implementer should re-derive each register's content at cycle n from its load expression at cycle n−1 before committing. (2) All new registers must sit inside the if (new_data_rdy) branch; putting any of them outside it breaks equivalence whenever new_data_rdy is low. (3) The 8.1 ns figure assumes median routing for the register→block and adder→enable-mux hops; those two wires are ~3.5 ns of the stage and are not under the rewrite's control, so the realized period may land between 8.1 and 8.7 ns — the tile reduction keeps the product below the current result across that range. (4) For f2/h2 the ×4 taps must stay a shift: write them as the explicit concatenation {9'b0, (din + n_delay_reg6), 2'b00} rather than *4, so synthesis cannot re-infer a multiplier and claim another tile.

first: area spread 0.0, period spread 0.2894 ns across the seeds.
